I. 
Introduction 
1 
Q. 
Please introduce the members of the Revenue Requirements Panel. 
2 
A. 
The Panel consists of James M. Molloy, David B. Doxsee, and Stephanie 
3 
A. Briggs. 
4 
 
5 
Q. 
Is this the Panel that testified previously in this proceeding?  
6 
A. 
Yes.  This Panel provided direct testimony as part of the January 29, 2016 
7 
and April 4, 2016 filings by KEDNY.  The terms defined in the Panel’s 
8 
direct testimony have the same definitions here.   
9 
 
10 
Q. 
What is the purpose of your rebuttal testimony? 
11 
A. 
The Panel responds to various adjustments proposed by Staff and 
12 
intervenors in their direct testimonies dated May 20, 2016.  In particular, 
13 
the Panel addresses the following subjects: (i) proposed revenue 
14 
adjustments; (ii) proposed O&M expense adjustments; (iii) regulatory 
15 
deferrals; (iv) proposed adjustments to taxes other than income taxes; (v) 
16 
rate base adjustments; and (vi) proposed changes to depreciation.  In 
17 
addition, the Panel addresses adjustments proposed by Staff that the 
18 
Company has previously agreed to adopt in information request (“IR”) 
19 
responses or agrees to adopt in rebuttal, and also addresses corrections to 
20 
Staff’s exhibits and testimony that the Company has identified.   
21 
3

Q. 
Does the Company respond to each of Staff’s and the intervenors’ 
1 
proposed adjustments and recommendations? 
2 
A. 
No.  In this testimony, as well as the rebuttal testimony of the other 
3 
Company witnesses, the Company is only responding to certain issues.  
4 
Where the Company accepts an adjustment or agrees with an issue, such 
5 
acceptance or agreement is explicitly acknowledged.  The Company’s 
6 
silence on a particular adjustment or recommendation should not be 
7 
construed as acceptance or waiver of a particular issue or as creating 
8 
precedent for any future proceeding.  The Company relies on its direct and 
9 
corrections and updates testimonies as support and justification for those 
10 
items not specifically addressed in its rebuttal testimony.   
11 
 
12

III. 
Operating Revenues Adjustments 
5 
Q.

6

Staff’s proposed adjustments to Operating Revenues are shown in Exhibit 
8

9 
 
10 
 
A. 
Site Investigation and Remediation 
11 
Q. 
Please summarize KEDNY’s proposal for recovery of SIR costs. 
12 
A. 
KEDNY proposed to include in base rates forecast SIR costs, excluding 
13 
the costs associated with the Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek sites.  
14 
KEDNY further proposed to modify the SIR Recovery Surcharge to 
15 
include annually: 
16 
 one-tenth of the forecast SIR deferral balance; and  
17 
 an amount capped at no more than a 2.5 percent increase in KEDNY’s 
18 
aggregate revenues measured from the prior year, consisting of (i) the 
19 
difference between actual SIR costs and the amount set in rates and (ii) 
20 
any amount not recovered in the prior year’s surcharge because that 
21 
7


--- Page 10 ---
Case 16-G-0059 
 
Rebuttal Testimony of the Revenue Requirements Panel 
 
Page 6 of 35 
amount would have increased KEDNY’s aggregate revenues by more 
1 
than 2.5 percent. 
2 
Costs for Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek would be recovered 
3 
through the SIR Recovery Surcharge reconciliation.   
4 
 
5 
Q. 
Does Staff agree with KEDNY’s proposal? 
6 
A. 
Staff agreed with the inclusion of forecast Rate Year SIR costs of $53.872 
7 
million (exclusive of Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek) in base rates, 
8 
but modified the Company’s proposal to also include one-tenth of the 
9 
deferral amortization amount of $18.521 million in base rates, resulting in 
10 
a base rate allowance of $72.393 million for SIR costs.  Staff did not agree 
11 
with the Company’s proposal to utilize the SIR Recovery Surcharge for 
12 
prospective recovery of the costs associated with the Gowanus Canal and 
13 
Newtown Creek sites, citing the uncertainty of the forecasts for those sites.  
14 
Instead, Staff recommended that KEDNY file a petition setting the 
15 
specific level of costs to recover through the SIR Recovery Surcharge 
16 
once the costs for Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek became more 
17 
defined. 
18 
 
19 
 
Although it is not explicit in Staff’s testimony, KEDNY assumes its 
20 
authority to maintain the current SIR deferral, which provides for full 
21 
8


--- Page 11 ---
Case 16-G-0059 
 
Rebuttal Testimony of the Revenue Requirements Panel 
 
Page 7 of 35 
reconciliation of SIR costs (except for the Coney Island and Citizens 
1 
sites), will continue unchanged (except for application of the WACC 
2 
instead of the AFUDC rate) under Staff’s proposal and that KEDNY is 
3 
permitted to defer the Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek costs. 
4 
 
5 
Q. 
Please comment on Staff’s proposal. 
6 
A. 
KEDNY agrees with Staff’s proposed base rate treatment of forecast SIR 
7 
costs and the deferral amortization, but does not agree with Staff’s 
8 
position on the SIR Recovery Surcharge.  While the Company agrees that 
9 
there is some uncertainty with respect to the estimate of future costs for 
10 
Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek, there is no dispute that these costs 
11 
will be significant and will add to an already large deferral balance.   
12 
 
13 
The Company’s SIR Recovery Surcharge proposal is designed to address 
14 
both the uncertainty raised by Staff as well as the potential magnitude of 
15 
the costs.  Under the Company’s proposal, costs KEDNY incurs with 
16 
respect to the Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek sites would not be 
17 
included in rates, but reconciled through the SIR Recovery Surcharge.  
18 
The Company does not propose to recover through the SIR Recovery 
19 
Surcharge costs forecast, but not yet incurred, thereby addressing any 
20 
uncertainty regarding the forecast costs.  This mechanism would provide 
21 
9


--- Page 12 ---
Case 16-G-0059 
 
Rebuttal Testimony of the Revenue Requirements Panel 
 
Page 8 of 35 
the Company with timely recovery of significant SIR costs, mitigate future 
1 
buildup of the deferral balance, and avoid future rate shock to customers.  
2 
While KEDLI is willing to eliminate its SIR Recovery Surcharge as Staff 
3 
recommends because it does not currently anticipate significant deviations 
4 
from its forecast of costs in base rates, KEDNY fully expects that its costs 
5 
to remediate the Gowanus and Newtown Creek sites, which are excluded 
6 
from base rates, will be significant and believes its proposal to recover 
7 
these costs through the SIR Recovery Surcharge is reasonable and in the 
8 
best interest of customers.  
9 
 
10 
Q. 
Please address PULP’s recommendation with respect to SIR costs. 
11 
A. 
PULP claims that shareholders should bear 20 percent of future SIR costs 
12 
to, among other things, provide an incentive to management to minimize 
13 
costs.  PULP, however, cites no evidence or analysis to support this claim.  
14 
While PULP makes a passing reference to the section of the 
15 
Commission’s SIR Generic Order that sets forth the reasons utility 
16 
shareholders may be held responsible for a portion of SIR costs, it cites 
17 
neither of them to support its recommendation.  Indeed, KEDNY’s cost 
18 
control efforts are well documented, follow the Commission’s best 
19 
practices inventory set forth in the SIR Generic Order, and have been 
20 
reviewed by Staff in this and other proceedings and found to be 
21 
10


--- Page 13 ---
Case 16-G-0059 
 
Rebuttal Testimony of the Revenue Requirements Panel 
 
Page 9 of 35 
reasonable.  PULP conducted no discovery on the Company’s cost 
1 
mitigation strategies and procurement practices and offered no testimony 
2 
to suggest the Company’s practices were inadequate or imprudent.  In fact, 
3 
PULP’s only argument, a blanket statement that shareholders should, in 
4 
equity, bear a portion of costs because of their role through successive 
5 
changes in management in creating and assuming SIR liabilities, was 
6 
rejected by the Commission in the SIR Generic Order.  
7 
 
8 
 
B. 
Merchant Function Charge 
9 
Q. 
What is Staff’s recommendation regarding the MFC? 
10 
A. 
Staff proposed an adjustment to KEDNY’s MFC revenue to align with the 
11 
Company’s MFC expense.  As explained in the rebuttal testimony of the 
12 
Rate Design Panel, Staff’s proposed adjustment does not affect overall 
13 
costs to or revenue from customers and the Company accepts the 
14 
adjustment.     
15 
 
16 
 
C. 
New York Facilities Surcharge 
17 
Q. 
What is Staff’s recommendation regarding the NYFS? 
18 
A. 
Staff recommends that the NYFS the Company proposed for recovering 
19 
the costs of joint facilities should not be adopted on the grounds that the 
20 
agreement governing joint facilities costs is currently being renegotiated 
21 
11


--- Page 14 ---
Case 16-G-0059 
 
Rebuttal Testimony of the Revenue Requirements Panel 
 
Page 10 of 35 
and is too uncertain.  Instead, Staff proposes that the Company continue to 
1 
recover its facilities costs through base rates and should file a petition with 
2 
the Commission seeking alternative ratemaking for the revenues received 
3 
and costs incurred once the updated agreement is finalized.  As discussed 
4 
in the Rate Design Panel’s rebuttal testimony, does not accept this 
5 
proposal.     
6 
 
7 
 
D. 
Energy Efficiency and System Benefits Charges 
8 
Q. 
What does Staff recommend regarding Energy Efficiency (“EE”) and 
9 
the SBC? 
10 
A. 
Staff Witness Mammen recommends adjustments to SBC revenues and 
11 
matching adjustments to EE expenses to reflect the results of recent 
12 
Commission orders in Cases 14-M-0094 and 15-M-0252.  These 
13 
adjustments do not impact the Company’s revenue requirement or the 
14 
amounts recovered from customers in the Rate Year and the Company 
15 
accepts them.  Staff also accepts the Company’s proposal to recover the 
16 
costs of internal labor associated with EE programs in base rates rather 
17 
than through the SBC.   
18 
 
19 
Q. 
Does the Company have any additional adjustments to EE labor? 
20 
A. 
Yes.  The Company is making a downward adjustment to Rate Year EE 
21 
12


--- Page 15 ---
Case 16-G-0059 
 
Rebuttal Testimony of the Revenue Requirements Panel 
 
Page 11 of 35 
labor and labor adders of $0.175 million to remove costs that were 
1 
overstated in the Historic Test Year, as provided in Exhibit ___ (RRP-7R). 
2 
 
3 
Q. 
Please explain the reason for the Company’s adjustment.  
4 
A. 
The Company derived its Rate Year labor forecast using employees on 
5 
payroll as of September 30, 2015, and the associated labor was allocated 
6 
to KEDNY using the accounting allocations in the Historic Test Year.  As 
7 
described in the rebuttal testimony of KEDLI’s Revenue Requirements 
8 
Panel, the suspension of KEDLI’s EE program in early 2015 resulted in 
9 
the diversion of costs to KEDNY in the Historic Test Year that it is not 
10 
expected to incur in the Rate Year.   
11 
 
12 
Q. 
What analysis did the Company perform to support its adjustment? 
13 
A. 
The Company analyzed KEDLI’s EE labor costs for the periods of 
14 
calendar year 2014 and the Historic Test Year.  The results show that 
15 
$0.093 million more in EE base labor was charged to KEDNY in the 
16 
Historic Test Year as a result of KEDLI’s program suspension.  However, 
17 
given that KEDLI’s EE program will resume by the beginning of the Rate 
18 
Year, these costs should be removed from KEDNY’s cost of service to 
19 
avoid overstating Rate Year expense.  Exhibit ___ (RRP-7R) shows the 
20 
calculation of the adjustment. 
21 
13


--- Page 16 ---
Case 16-G-0059 
 
Rebuttal Testimony of the Revenue Requirements Panel 
 
Page 12 of 35 
 
1 
 
E. 
Low Income Program  
2 
Q. 
What is the Company’s response to Staff’s recommendation 
3 
regarding the Low Income Program? 
4 
A. 
As discussed in the rebuttal testimony of the Shared Services Panel, the 
5 
changes in low income rates required by the Commission’s recent Order in 
6 
Case 14-M-0565 are not expected to be implemented until January 1, 2018 
7 
at the earliest—which is after the Rate Year—and the Company maintains 
8 
that its low income discount proposal is reasonable in the interim.  
9 
Therefore, the five percent increased benefit level and associated low 
10 
income program revenue requirement in the Rate Year are unchanged 
11 
from the Company’s direct filing.     
12 
 
13 
Q. 
What is Staff’s recommendation regarding how Low Income Program 
14 
costs should be recovered? 
15 
A. 
Staff recommends that the costs of the Low Income Program be recovered 
16 
in base delivery rates following the revenue allocation and rate design 
17 
process to recover the total revenue requirement approved in this case.  
18 
Differences between actual and projected Low Income Program costs 
19 
would be deferred for later disposition.   
20

A. 
Contractors 
6 
Q. 
Did Staff propose adjustments to contractors expense? 
7 
A. 
Yes.  Staff proposes one normalizing adjustment to contractors expense 
8 
that would reduce the Company’s revenue requirement by $0.578 million. 
9 
 
10 
Q. 
Please discuss Staff’s adjustment. 
11 
A. 
Staff generally agrees with the methodology used by the Company to 
12 
calculate contractors expense.  However, Staff recommends removing 
13 
several charges associated with ULC Robotics, Inc. in the Historic Test 
14 
Year.  In its response to IR DPS-422 (MEW-32), a copy of which is 
15 
included in Staff’s Exhibit __ (SAP-3), the Company recognized the basis 
16 
for Staff’s proposed adjustment and agrees with it.   
17 
 
18 
 
B. 
Other Expense 
19 
Q. 
Did Staff propose adjustments to other expense? 
20 
A. 
Yes.  Staff proposes one normalizing adjustment to other expense that 
21 
16


--- Page 19 ---
Case 16-G-0059 
 
Rebuttal Testimony of the Revenue Requirements Panel 
 
Page 15 of 35 
reduces the Company’s revenue requirements by $1.098 million. 
1 
 
2 
Q. 
What is the basis for the proposed adjustment? 
3 
A. 
Staff’s adjustment removes charges associated with violations and fines 
4 
incurred by KEDNY for work performed in New York City.  Staff also 
5 
recommends that adjustments be made to remove the violations and fines 
6 
from the Company’s Rate Year rate base and associated depreciation 
7 
expense.  This recommendation results in three additional adjustments:  
8 
1. 
A downward adjustment of $3.252 million to net plant; 
9 
2. 
An upward adjustment of $0.865 million to accumulated deferred 
10 
income taxes; and 
11 
3. 
A downward adjustment to depreciation expense of $0.063 million. 
12 
 
13 
Q. 
Does the Company agree with Staff’s adjustment? 
14 
A. 
No.  In response to IR DPS-485 (MEW-33), a copy of which is included in 
15 
Staff’s Exhibit __ (SAP-3), the Company explained that these costs are 
16 
incurred in its ordinary course of business as the Company must regularly 
17 
excavate in streets and sidewalks in the City of New York.  The Company 
18 
takes precautions to avoid such fines and violations, if practical; however, 
19 
given the nature of many of the Company’s projects, these costs are not 
20 
cost-effectively avoidable.  For example, the Company could take 
21 
17


--- Page 20 ---
Case 16-G-0059 
 
Rebuttal Testimony of the Revenue Requirements Panel 
 
Page 16 of 35 
measures, such as repeated demobilizations and remobilizations, to 
1 
mitigate the charges, but such measures would be more costly than the 
2 
amounts incurred in fines and violations.  Thus, while the Company will 
3 
continue its efforts to avoid fines and violations to the extent possible, it 
4 
believes that these charges are a reasonable cost of doing business in its 
5 
service territory and should be recoverable from customers.  
6 
 
7 
 
C. 
Service Company Rent Expense 
8 
Q. 
Does Staff make any adjustments to Service Company rents? 
9 
A. 
Yes.  Staff proposes three adjustments to Service Company rents. 
10 
 
11 
Q. 
Please describe Staff’s first adjustment. 
12 
A. 
Staff proposes removing the costs of the GTIS project.  The adjustment 
13 
results in a reduction in Service Company rent expense of $1.416 million 
14 
for KEDNY.  Staff’s adjustment is based on the Company’s delays in 
15 
implementing the system.  Once the system is implemented, Staff 
16 
recommends that costs be revisited and a determination made as to how 
17 
much should be allocated to customers in a future rate case. 
18 
 
19 
Q.

E. 
Transportation Lease Expense 
13 
Q. 
Please describe Staff’s proposed adjustment to the Company’s 
14 
forecast of transportation lease expense. 
15 
A. 
Staff proposes a downward adjustment of $0.732 million to Rate Year 
16 
transportation lease expense.  However, Staff’s methodology to forecast 
17 
transportation lease expense omits significant transportation lease costs the 
18 
Company expects to incur in the Rate Year and Staff’s proposed 
19 
adjustment should be rejected.   
20 
 
21 
22


--- Page 25 ---
Case 16-G-0059 
 
Rebuttal Testimony of the Revenue Requirements Panel 
 
Page 21 of 35 
Q. 
Please explain. 
1 
A. 
The methodology used by Staff, which uses Historic Test Year lease costs 
2 
plus inflation, then adds the lease costs for incremental vehicles plus 
3 
inflation, omits significant costs associated with replacement vehicles.  By 
4 
contrast, the Company’s vehicle-by-vehicle replacement analysis to 
5 
forecast Rate Year costs accounts for the projected cost of new vehicles in 
6 
accordance with current vehicle and equipment lifecycles.  Vehicle 
7 
inventory and associated lease expense vary on a monthly basis due to the 
8 
continuous replacement of vehicles and updating of lease terms.  As 
9 
explained in the response to IR DPS-429 (JPC-18), a copy of which is 
10 
included in Staff’s Exhibit __ (SAP-3), the Company forecast 
11 
transportation lease expense by starting with the vehicle inventory as of 
12 
September 30, 2015, and then adding, removing and modifying vehicles 
13 
and lease terms to reflect the expected changes to inventory in the link 
14 
period and through the Rate Year.  The forecast increase in lease expense 
15 
from September 30, 2015 to the Rate Year is driven largely by 
16 
replacement vehicles.  For example, as of the end of the Historic Test 
17 
Year, several vehicles were fully amortized and thus incurred only a small 
18 
nominal monthly fee (from $7 to $30).  These vehicles are forecast to be 
19 
replaced sometime after September 30, 2015 at a normal monthly lease 
20 
expense that is significantly higher than the nominal monthly fee.   
21 
23


--- Page 26 ---
Case 16-G-0059 
 
Rebuttal Testimony of the Revenue Requirements Panel 
 
Page 22 of 35 
 
1 
As described in the response to IR DPS-308 (JPC-12), a copy of which is 
2 
included in Staff’s Exhibit __ (SAP-3), KEDNY and KEDLI projected 
3 
that during the link period from the end of the Historic Test Year to the 
4 
Rate Year they would replace 295 vehicles (109 for KEDNY, 186 for 
5 
KEDLI).  Exhibit __ (RRP-3R), pages 2 – 7, show the Historic Test Year 
6 
lease costs of 253 of the 295 replacement vehicles, and that on average 
7 
225 of these vehicles were fully amortized in the Historic Test Year.  
8 
Exhibit __ (RRP-3R), page 8, shows that 41 of the 295 vehicles incurred 
9 
no lease cost in the Historic Test Year because they were either sold or 
10 
owned by the Companies.  Because the Historic Test Year lease costs 
11 
reflect the reduced costs associated with these fully amortized, sold or 
12 
owned vehicles, applying Staff’s methodology of using Historic Test Year 
13 
costs for these 295 vehicles, inflated, and not accounting for the costs of 
14 
replacement vehicles would significantly understate the anticipated Rate 
15 
Year expense.  Exhibit __ (RRP-3R), page 1, summarizes the difference in 
16 
forecast lease expense for the 295 replaced vehicles using Staff’s forecast 
17 
methodology and the Companies’ forecast methodology.   
18

targeted for replacement?

12

14

17

18

to Staff’s plant model in Staff’s response to IR BULI-5 (RRP-2).  Exhibit 
9 
__ (RRP-9R) includes a copy of Staff’s response to IR BULI-5 (RRP-2) 
10 
and a copy of IR BULI-7 (RRP-2).   Although the revenue requirement 
11 
supported by and reflected in the Company’s rebuttal case includes the 
12 
effects of the adjustments shown in IR BULI-7 (RRP-2), Staff has not yet 
13 
responded to all of the indicated adjustments at this time. 
14 
  
15 
X. 
Conclusion 
16 
Q. 
Does this conclude the Panel’s rebuttal testimony? 
17 
A. 
Yes.

SUBJECT:
Performance Excellence Program (PEX) Savings
Request:
In the testimony of the Revenue Requirements Panel, efficiency and productivity cost reductions
are described relating to the PEX organization (see, KEDNY pp. 92-100; KEDLI pp. 93-100).
The panel for both KEDNY and KEDLI stated on p. 92 and p. 93, respectively, that “National
Grid established the PEX organization to improve overall performance, mitigate costs and
enhance service to customers by simplifying and improving end-to-end processes across the
business.” PEX initiatives are classified into three types – Type I, Type II, Type III – based on
the potential benefits they could provide. PEX teams have broken down the initiatives into (a)
the “Top 35” most impactful initiatives and (b) “Other” initiatives that are considered to be
outside of the Top 35.
1) Provide a list of the “Top 35” initiatives, broken by initiative type (e.g. Type I, Type II,
Type III) and provide a detailed description of each.
For each initiative identified,
provide the initiative tracking form (similar to the workpapers provided in Exhibit __
(RRP-11) Schedule 34, Workpaper 3, pp. 1-25), and provide the backup calculations
demonstrating the expected annual savings amount when fully implemented, and the
various cost component elements the savings are expected to affect.
2) Provide a list of the “Other” initiatives, broken down by initiative type (e.g. Type I, Type
II, Type III), and provide a detailed description of each. For each initiative identified,
provide the initiative tracking form (similar to the workpapers provided in Exhibit __
(RRP-11) Schedule 34, Workpaper 3, pp. 1-25) and also provide the backup calculations

8.
Please see pages 12 to 38 of KEDNY’s Revenue Requirement Panel testimony (and
the related exhibits referenced therein) for a description of the comprehensive analyses

portion of SAP (EHR1) costs from expense to capital. Many of the vendors shown in
Part 6 are included in the capital/expense analysis. For the remaining charges/credits
greater than $100,000, most represent accruals and reversals within the historic test
year period resulting in a zero impact to overall consultant expense.
10.
The Company used costs charged to legal department cost centers to identify the costs
to include in the legal expense cost element. Because the law firm vendors included in
the consultant cost element are related to collection services and charged to the credit
and collection departments, these costs were not included in the legal expense cost
element and remained in consultant expense. As such, there was no double count of
expenses.

project, and by year. Provide the information separately for KEDLI and KEDNY.
Response:
1) and 2)
Please see Attachment 1 for the projects that comprise $17,804,287 million in total capital
spend for GTIS projects from FY2010 through February 2016. Please note that the
Companies erroneously provided a total capital spend of $17.369 million in response to DPS-
350. A total capital spend of $17,804,287 is reflected in the general ledger for GTIS projects,
including the Customer Choice ESCO Gas project.
In the course of preparing Attachment 1, the Companies identified certain existing Service
Company assets/projects that were not included in Exhibit __ (RRP-11-CU), Workpapers to
Exhibit ___ (RRP-CU), Schedule 9, Workpaper 2 for both KEDNY and KEDLI. Attachment
2 identifies the missing projects and the total rent expense impact to KEDNY and KEDLI.
The missing projects included three work orders for the GTIS project performed as part of
investments INVP 1182 and INVP 1182B that delivered an early version of GTIS.
